    tools/anim_shape_bench.sh     the shape-vs-known-corpus measurement above
    tools/anim_corpus_battery.sh  corpus-only products (leaves/prefixes/tokens), incl.
                                  the 2e10 leaf^3; all 0 new
    tools/anim_mutate_loop.sh     cascading mutate, seeds and tokens both grow from the
                                  finds; saturates on pass 1

  What would break the saturation, in order of promise: (a) a tooltip or asset-browser
  view of any *one* remaining clip, which turns the vocabulary problem into a shape
  problem; (b) the unit/state-machine body itself, since clips are named after the state
  that plays them (#47.9) and `enumerate_anims.py`'s 55 M stream over the Lua event names
  is a true zero (its space *does* contain the names we hold, so the negative is real;
  `--candidates` was verified with a known pair for exactly this reason); (c) the same
  cross-build hunt the icons used - a 2014/2015 snapshot's animation names for hashes that
  are still live today.

  64.7 THE SHARED CLIPS: THE CONCEPT IS SHARED, THE SPELLING IS NOT

  61.2/64.2 asked the right question and the corpus answers it: an animation *is* shared
  between entities - the same walk, the same hit, the same die - but never as the same
  string.  Grouping the 590 resolved leaves into concepts makes the size of the problem
  visible: **452 concepts, 62 of them spelled more than one way**.

    hit_offset    hit_offset, hit_offset1, hit_offset2, hit_offset3, hit_offset01,
                  hit_offset02, hit_offset03, hit_offset_01, ..., offset_hit_01   (11)
    die / death   die_left_01, die_left1, die_left_2, death_left_01, death_left_02,
                  die_headshot01, death_headshot_02, die_decapitated
    move / walk   move_walk_backwards (mummy_risen) beside move_walk_back (mummy_priest),
                  move_run, walk_run, walk_default, walk_strafe_left

  So "does this entity have the clip the others have?" is not a string search, it is a
  search *per spelling convention* - which is what `tools/anim_shared_vocab.py` does: the
  corpus's concepts, respelled every way the corpus respells them (underscored number,
  glued number, `_default`/`_normal`/`_light`/`_heavy`, direction tokens with and without
  numbers, reversed token order so `offset_hit_01` is reachable), under the directories of
  the 37 entities that still own unresolved clips.

  It named four clips in one run - all on the **singular** `animation/` directory, all
  with the number **glued**:

    characters/cultist_novice/animation/die_headshot01   fdaf5c89e291301c
    characters/cultist_novice/animation/die_left01       23cc119b8b8ddb09
    characters/cultist_novice/animation/die_right01      910ca8a9e30041d7
    characters/cultist_novice/animation/die_slump01      0a9ed352aeb62d9b

  None of the four was reachable by anything built before, and the reason is structural,
  not budgetary: `mutate` substitutes a *token*, so it can turn `die_headshot_01` into
  `die_headshot_1` but can never drop the separator; `%p_%t` and `leaf_tail` always join
  with `_`; and the glued shapes (`%a%b`) glue *everything*, so the mixed form
  `<words>_<words><NN>` - which is what these are - has no expression in the pattern
  language at all.  Widening the conventions (26.8 M candidates: numbers 1-20 in four
  forms, more suffix families, both directions) added nothing, so the four are the whole
  of this family's spelling problem.

  Two other fingerprints were aimed at the same targets and both are recorded as
  negatives, because each would have been decisive had it held:

    * **identical bodies**: 0 of the 307 unresolved animation bodies are byte-identical to
      any of the 939 named ones.  An animation is rigged to its entity, so a shared clip
      does not share bytes and the name is not recoverable from them.
    * **size twins**: 116 unresolved rows (48 hashes) have a named animation of exactly the
      same `body_size`, and size is nearly a function of the clip (#43).  The twin is not
      the answer either - 9,086 candidates built from those twins (in the entity's own
      directory, respelled) are 0 - and the biggest clusters show why: size 444 is
      `stage1_idle`/`stage2_idle`/`stage3_idle`/`dormant` for `gameobjects/spawners`, and
      the unresolved clips at that size are *other* clips of the same shape, not those
      clips under another name.

  What the referrers do give, reliably, is the *directory* - and they have to be read as
  paths, not as unit names.  `gameobjects/spawners/spawner_beam` alone owns 141 of the 543
  unresolved rows, and the named spawner clips all sit in one shared directory
  (`gameobjects/spawners/animations/`: `stage1_idle`, `stage2_idle`, `stage3_idle`,
  `stage1_hit`, `stage2_hit`, `stage3_hit`, `death`, `appear`, `dormant`).  Deriving only
  the first-level unit collapses that name away, so `tools/anim_entity_guess.py` walks
  every progressive context (`<each prefix>/{animations,animation}`) crossed with the
  corpus leaves, the size twins and their respellings: 117,890 candidates, 0.  The spawner
  clips are not a stage matrix either (`stage<N>_<verb>` and `<spawner>_<verb>` over 50
  verbs, 9,870 candidates, 0).

  State after this round: overlay 6,899 names, 17,886/20,004 rows named, 909 unique
  unresolved hashes; the animation bucket 309 -> 305 distinct (543 unresolved rows, 141 of
  them spawner-owned).

65. UNITS: READ THE BODY, AND READ THE TREE AS IT IS

  The animation work kept stalling on one thing: the directory.  `characters/<unit>/animations`
  is only right when the unit *is* the entity - and 42 unresolved hashes are `unit`s
  themselves (54 rows), all in level modules (`modules/d01_crypt/resources`,
  `modules/d01_lava/resources`, `modules/menu/resources`).  These are the modular level
  geometry, and a unit's name *is* its animation directory, so naming a unit names a
  directory.

  65.1 THE BODY NAMES ITS OWN PIECES

  A Stingray unit body embeds its node names as plain ASCII - material nodes (`m_*`),
  sections (`s_*`), walkable zones (`z_*`).  Reading them out of
  `~/Documents/Coding/Gauntlet-Export/unresolved/unit/<hash>.unit` gives, for example:

    1f01e19947211248   m_crypt_railing_01, s_crypt_railing_straight_2c
    34ad7dab78fa1dbf   m_crypt_railing_01, s_crypt_railing_end
    05b95a956d2b2f06   m_lavastruct_floor_02, m_lavastruct_pillar_01, m_lavastruct_railing_01
    2e5618907cc69809   z_platform_walkable_01 ... _11
    71d81979402a0c01   m_lavastruct_goat_satan_statue
    93f1d4141e624ab2   m_lava_warterfall_01, m_lava_waterfall_02

  The `s_` strings are the *pieces*.  The `m_` strings are materials, and they sit close to
  the unit name without being it: `m_lavastruct_railing_01` belongs to the unit
  `blocks/d01_lava/railings/lavastruct_railing_2c`, so the trailing `_01` drops and a corner
  suffix (`_2c`/`_4c`, the corner count) appears.  (Note `warterfall` - the typo is in the
  game's own asset.)

  65.2 THE THREE NAMES

    blocks/d01_crypt/railings/crypt_railing_end          34ad7dab78fa1dbf
    blocks/d01_crypt/railings/crypt_railing_straight_2c  1f01e19947211248
    blocks/d01_crypt/railings/crypt_railing_straight_4c  b04f8e8773161d9d

  All three are the *literal* `s_*` strings under `blocks/<level>/<family>/`.  `railings`
  had never appeared in our known `d01_crypt` names - the family dirs we held for that level
  were only pillars/borders/stairs/rocks/gigameshes/floors/walls - because no *named*
  resource had ever put a file there.  So deriving the family list *per level from our own
  corpus* silently excluded the one directory the answer lived in: that trap is what made two
  passes of 1.6 M and 705 K candidates return 0.  What works is the union of families across
  every level (plus the real unit-directory tree read out of the catalogue), which is what
  `tools/unit_guess_by_body.py` now does.  Its control - a copy of the catalogue with those
  three names blanked - reproduces all three (3,309,852 candidates, 3 matches).

  65.3 THE NEGATIVES

    * `m_` strings are usually *not* the unit name: `crypt_floor_01` is a texture
      (`blocks/d01_crypt/crypt_floor_01_diff`) whose unit we still have not named.
    * Only ~30% of these units carry any `s_`/`z_` string at all (18 of 54 rows), so the
      method cannot reach the rest on its own.
    * The referrer route is empty here: `modules/d01_lava/room_lava_floor_adventure_2_template`
      and the `crypt_quilt_*` encounter templates reference their pieces **by hash**, and
      their bodies embed only markers and connectors (`north2_open`, `south2_closed`,
      `marker_unit`, `encounter_node_start`) - the pieces' names are not written down there.
    * `Gauntlet-install/Gauntlet-Source-DO_NOT_EDIT` is Lua-only (927 files: lua, foundation,
      gameobjects, gui, characters, equipment, ability_units, props) and has no `blocks/`
      tree, so the level geometry was never shipped as source text.
    * Stem x every known unit directory (10,270), stem x respellings x every known unit
      directory (705,312) and the literal `s_`/`z_` stems x every known unit directory
      (34,286) are all 0 - the directory, not the leaf, was the unknown.
    * The 141 spawner-owned animation rows survive the same treatment: the concept-spelling
      vocabulary (which named the four cultist clips, 64.7) crossed with every spawner
      directory context (`gameobjects/spawners/{animations,animation}` and
      `gameobjects/spawners/spawner_{beam,twister,t1,ghost}/{animations,animation}`) is
      4,346,808 candidates, 0.

  State after this round: overlay 6,902 names, 17,892/20,004 rows named, 906 unique
  unresolved hashes; unit bucket 42 -> 39.

66. THE BUCKETS THAT DID NOT MOVE, AND WHY

  66.1 MORE FINDS.  Re-running the standing battery (`sweeps/battery.sh`) after the three
  units were named has now produced **five** names - the batch of new names is fuel for
  phases that had been exhausted at the previous corpus size:

    fc65a8a7da42eacc  effects/trap_floor_blades_swipe
    7701c677b29b25e0  effects/wep_valkyrie_ultimate_vengeance_slash
    a0c3772f0de799e6  base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_level:diffuse_map:normal_map
    71f47829a45b0f5d  base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_other:diffuse_map:static_lighting
    75fe5327ac8d781b  base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_prop:diffuse_map:normal_map

  Two of them are effects and three are shader-library *flag chains*, so the chain
  enumeration of section 60.14 was not complete either - worth remembering before any
  future round is called closed.

  66.2 TEXTURES (252 distinct unresolved).  All of these are 0:

    * byte-identical bodies - 0 of 252 against 1,540 named texture bodies, so the same art is
      never shipped twice under the same bytes in this build.
    * pixel-identical PNG twins (`tools/twin_by_image.py`, the oracle that caught the ghost
      orb).  Exactly one exact match, `72363b5263f238e0` == `textures/white`; the 47
      near-twins under 0.02 are almost all flat `_norm` maps (all flat normals look alike),
      and filtering the twins to diffuse art leaves 36 hints whose family mutation is 0.
    * the map-set rule - an owner's *known* textures give the channel set and the unresolved
      sibling is another channel: 162,871 candidates, 0.  Owners reference generic shared art
      (`textures/outline`, `menu/black`, `effects/textures/alpha_smoke_thin_01`) while their
      own art is a word we do not hold.
    * the node names inside the *owner's* body (`m_`/`s_`/`t_` stems) x the owner's directory
      x channel suffixes: 12,860, 0; the same with every known texture directory as the dir
      side: 890,807, 0.
    * `crack_siblings.py --type texture`: 31 M candidates, 0.

  66.3 MATERIALS (91).  Owner bodies plus every material directory: 2,137,379 candidates, 0.
  The known materials are named after their *units* (`blocks/d01_caves/caverock_set`,
  `ability_units/spawners/spawner_exploding_burning`), so an unresolved material is the
  material of a unit we have not named - the dependency runs unit -> material, not the other
  way round.

  66.4 `tools/twin_by_refs.py --write` over the whole residual: 661 targets had a twin,
  1,484,090 candidates, 0.

  66.5 THE COMMON CAUSE is the one from 64.5: these words are in no vocabulary we hold, and
  every method above can only reshuffle a name we already have.  What breaks the wall is a
  *body* (65.1, three units) or a *spelling convention* (64.7, four clips) - both live in the
  assets and not in our word lists.

  66.6 NEW SWEEP.  `sweeps/find_new.sh` runs this session's four finders in sequence
  (`anim_shared_vocab`, `unit_guess_by_body`, `anim_twin_by_size`, `anim_mutate_loop`) and
  merges into the overlay.  Like the standing battery, it is meant to be re-run after every
  batch of new names.

  66.7 WHICH BODIES ACTUALLY CARRY NAMES.  The "read the body" route (65.1) only exists where
  a body stores names as plain ASCII, so every type was audited for it - bodies that contain
  at least one `name`-shaped string (lower-case, `_` or `/` separated):

    type            bodies  with names   strings   what they are
    shader_library     362         357     7,084   parameter names (ambient_top_color), not
                                                    resource names
    shader              64          64       825   the same
    unit                48          48       119   node names - these *are* the names (65.1)
    texture            672          40        80   DDS header noise
    timpani_bank        37          28       135   Ogg stream noise
    level               12          12        75   references, i.e. real names - but only of
                                                    resources we already hold (0 hits over the
                                                    679 level bodies, all 60 distinct strings
                                                    already resolved)
    flow                 4           4        12
    animation          539           0         0
    particles          161           0         0
    material           207           0         0
    package              2           0         0

  So the route is closed for animations, particles, materials and packages - their bodies are
  rigged motion, DXBC, shader parameter blocks and hashed tables, none of which spell a name.
  It is open exactly where it paid: units.

  66.8 SHADER LIBRARY PARAMETERS DO NOT GIVE THE CHAIN.  A library body does carry its
  parameter names, and those correlate with the flags in its name (`diffuse_map` ->
  `diffuse_map`, `billboard` -> `c_billboard`, `static_lighting` -> `static_lighting`), so
  the flags were recovered from the parameters and scored against the 453 known libraries:
  recall 0.831, but only **5 of 453** libraries are recovered *exactly*, and an unresolved
  body proposes ~62 flags - the marker sets are shared globals (`ambient_top_color`,
  `c_per_object`) rather than discriminators.  Too broad to build a chain from; closed.

67. THE VFX AND WEAPON HUNT: THE EFFECT SENTENCE IS BUILT FROM ABILITIES

  67.1 WHERE THE GAPS ARE.  The grouped view of section 66.6 showed the residual to be VFX
  and hero equipment - exactly as it looks from the type totals:

    resource_packages/all_effects               171 rows  material 69, particles 68, texture 32, shader 2
    resource_packages/heroes/necromagus_ability  39       animation 14, material 7, particles 7, shader 2, texture 9
    resource_packages/heroes/wizard_base         39       animation 26, material 3, particles 3, texture 7
    resource_packages/heroes/warrior_base        38       animation 25, material 3, particles 3, texture 7
    resource_packages/heroes/necromagus_base     34       animation 17, material 5, particles 5, texture 7
    resource_packages/heroes/valkyrie_base       29       animation 15, material 3, particles 3, texture 8
    resource_packages/heroes/elf_base            28       animation 15, material 3, particles 3, texture 7
    resource_packages/heroes/warrior_equipment   15       material 6, particles 6, shader 1, texture 2
    resource_packages/heroes/*_capes             3 each   texture 3

  Reading the bundles rather than counting them gave two corrections that matter.  In
  `all_effects` the **material and the particles share one name and one hash** - 69 against 68
  is the same resources counted in two type slots, so one effect name pays twice.  And the
  three unresolved textures in *each* of the five cape packs are the **same three hashes**
  (2,796,372 / 699,220 / 699,220 bytes): one cape set, shipped once, referenced by every hero.

  67.2 THE EIGHT NAMES.  An effect name is a sentence -
  `effects/<prefix>_<faction>_<ability>_<phase>` -

    effects/cha_cultist_sorcerer_beam_windup
    effects/boss_circle_turn_outer

  and `tools/effects_family.py` already sweeps that shape with the **unit** vocabulary (its
  stems are unit directories).  That is the wrong half of the sentence: it scored 0 across
  all ten prefixes, ~2.7 M candidates each.  Driving the same shape with the **ability** names
  we already hold - every leaf under `characters/*/abilities/`, `*/animations/`, `*/weapons/`
  plus every prefix and suffix of those leaves - named eight in one run
  (`tools/effects_by_ability.py`):

    7d18b518b2650be4  effects/wep_elf_arrow_snipershot_pierce_trail
    1df09ec7da3a8fd2  effects/wep_necromagus_heavy_shadowbolt_hit
    8e05da3e161ce35e  effects/wep_necromagus_heavy_shadowbolt_trail
    ab620c86ce595d70  effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_remnant_spawn
    a3656cd886df16ea  effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_souleater_trail
    d62a9c93e91c2b9d  effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_remnant_idle
    35565841792929ea  effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_remnant_explode
    48f1d20eb9fa6cdc  effects/cha_spider_queen_egg_projectile_trail

  Eight names cleared 34 rows: 17,912 -> 17,946 named (89.71%), 900 -> 892 unique.  The
  rounding is the material/particles doubling and the several packs each effect ships in, and
  the referrer graph confirms the shape from the other side - `effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_souleater_trail`
  is referenced by `characters/avatar_necromagus/abilities/ultimate_souleater`.

  67.3 CLOSED, WITH NUMBERS.  Every one of these is 0 against the residual:

    * the *item* half of the sentence: `effects/<p>_<faction>_<item>_<tail>`,
      `effects/<p>_<item>_<tail>` and `effects/<faction>_<item>_<tail>` with 113 weapon and
      equipment stems - the weapon VFX are not named after the weapon.
    * a second tail token (`%t_%t` and `%t%t`) on the five heroes, and ability-before-faction
      (`effects/<p>_<ability>_<faction>_<tail>`).
    * all 19 `effects/<p>_` prefixes rather than the main ten - the space is exhausted.
    * every texture derivation from the *referrer*: `<referrer stem>_<channel>`, the
      referrer's own directory, `effects/textures/<stem>`, `<hero>/abilities/<stem>_<channel>`
      and `equipment/<hero>/<stem>_<channel>` (65,535 candidates).  An effect's texture is
      *shared art*, not a derivative of the effect's name.
    * channel completion over every equipment and effects name (70,859); the cape *meshes*'
      stems (they are `cape_s0N_r0M` and their textures are not `<stem>_<channel>`); the known
      effect-texture prefixes crossed with the known words and the pool; and
      `shaders/<effect>` / `effects/meshes/<effect>` (2,935).
    * the size-family rule on textures: 142 of the unresolved textures are exactly 349,684
      bytes, which is the size of the known `*_norm` maps - they are normal maps whose base
      names we do not hold either.  Size says *what kind* of art a texture is, never which.

  67.4 WHAT IS LEFT.  `all_effects` 155 rows (61 effect names + 32 shared textures + 2
  shaders); the five `*_equipment` packs (15/9/7/5); the five `*_capes` (3 each - three names
  would clear all fifteen); five hero `*_banks`, one audio bank each; and the hero `*_base`
  packs, which are animations (26/25/17/15/15) and belong to the animation bucket, not to VFX.

  State after this hunt: overlay 6,916 names, 17,946/20,004 rows named (89.71%), 892 unique
  unresolved hashes.

68. THE SHADER-CHAIN NEIGHBOURHOOD IS WORTH FIFTEEN NAMES

  68.1 WHY THE CHAINS ARE WORTH CHASING.  The grouped view (#66.6) puts 350 of the missing
  rows in three bundles - `core/rendering/renderer` 137, `mobile_renderer` 108,
  `emulate_renderer` 105 - and they are the *same* chains in all three, so one name pays three
  times.  A chain name is `<technique>:<flag>:<flag>...` with the flags alphabetically sorted
  (#41), so the space is enumerable: 43 techniques, 110 flags, chain lengths 2-10 (the corpus
  holds 88 chains of length 2, 66 of 3, 61 of 6, 56 of 5, 49 of 4, 41 of 7, 25 of 8).

  68.2 WHAT FOUND THEM.  `tools/make_chain_candidates.py` - the project's own one-flag
  neighbourhood (insert, drop or swap one flag, re-sorted) - run **greedily**: its seeds are
  the chains we hold, so every find widens the next pass.  Four passes of ~5.3 M candidates:

    pass 1   6    character:double_sided:enemy:one_bit_alpha:skinned_3weights, ...
    pass 2   7    base:instanced:diffuse_map:material_map:normal_map, base:instanced:diffuse_map, ...
    pass 3   2    base:instanced:diffuse_map:self_illumination_map, ...
    pass 4   0    converged

  Fifteen names, and because each is live in three renderer bundles they cleared 39 rows:
  17,946 -> 17,985 named (89.91%), 892 -> 877 unique.

  68.3 CLOSED AFTERWARDS, WITH NUMBERS.  With the fifteen in the corpus the space was re-swept
  and everything reachable from here is exhausted: the same tool to convergence (pass 4); the
  same flag set under every other technique (`<tech>:<flags of a known chain>`, 141,581
  candidates - how a renderer variant would be found); the whole sorted-subset space of every
  technique's own flag vocabulary at lengths 2-4 (38,455); and the decal family with two free
  flags (`base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_*:<f1>:<f2>`, 141,581).  What is left of the
  shader bucket is 5+ flag chains, where a technique's size band (#60.14) is the only
  constraint - `hunt_shader_subsets.py`'s territory, already swept.

  68.4 NOT FOR WANT OF TRYING: THE ACHIEVEMENT ICONS.  The thirteen unresolved 43,836-byte
  textures are exactly the size of the `achievement_icons/*` pairs (`x` and `x_locked`), and
  achievement names are English titles (`as_luck_would_have_it`, `besting_the_beast_of_orox`,
  `captains_special`).  The *known* ones are only sporadically present in our mined strings
  (`bombardier` in `loc_words`/`strings_pool`/`bundle_strings`, `artful_dodger` in
  `bundle_strings` alone), and slugging every harvested string - 1,026,408 slugs, 3,079,204
  candidates, with apostrophes dropped the way the game drops them (`Captain's Special` ->
  `captains_special`) - finds none of the missing thirteen.  The achievement block is not in
  the strings we harvested.

  State: overlay 6,931 names, 17,985/20,004 rows named (89.91%), 877 unique unresolved.

69. MUTATE, SIZED RIGHT, AND WHAT THE LAST LOOK ESTABLISHED

  69.1 MUTATE'S COST MODEL, WHICH IS EASY TO GET WRONG.  `mutate` does `tokens x suffixes`
  *per seed* - not per run - so a "big pool" run can be astronomically large before the
  engine says anything (`--tokens` 245,000 with `--suffixes` 10,661 against 1,432 seeds is
  1.5e11 candidates and never returns; the right-sized version, 2,000 x 200, is 5.7e8 and
  finishes).  All of these are 0: the package-scoped `pkg_mutate.sh` over the biggest
  packages, a new `sweeps/mutate_from_refs.sh` that builds **referrer-placed seeds** in the
  target's own namespace (1,432 seeds - effect textures under `effects/textures/`, ability art
  under `characters/<hero>/abilities/`, shaders under `shaders/`, per type) with the
  anim-token, strings-pool, tokens_big, `all_names` and `ic_all` pools and a wide `all_tails`
  suffix pool (10,661 tails mined from every leaf token we hold), and the self-feeding global
  mutate (all 6,329 held names as seeds).  The placement was right; the *stems* are the wall.

  69.2 THE ANIMATION RESIDUAL IS ONE FAMILY.  Grouping the unresolved animations by their
  referrer shows where they really live: 184 of them are referenced out of the `debug` bundle
  (the audit falls back to the bundle name when the referring resource is itself unnamed),
  then the hero base packs.  And their sizes say which family: 332 bytes x40, 696 x17, 444 x16.
  Exactly one known animation is 332 bytes - `equipment/elf/animations/aim` and `drawn` - and
  444/696 are the spawner clips (`stage1_idle` ... `stage3_idle`, `dormant`, `stage6_hit`).
  So the largest unresolved animation cluster is aim/draw-class clips and spawner stage clips
  for entities whose *names* we do not hold.

  69.3 MY DIRECTORY LIST HAD A SCOPE GAP - AND FIXING IT DID NOT HELP.  Every animation sweep
  so far used directories derived from the *referrers* (`anim_dirs_ctx.txt`), which is why
  `equipment/<hero>/animations/` was never tried even though `equipment/elf/animations/aim`
  is a name we hold.  The fix - every prefix of every known name crossed with `animations`
  and `animation`, times the corpus leaves, the ability stems and number respellings - is
  0.  The gap was real; it just was not the obstacle.

  69.4 WHAT THE BLOCKS AND PROPS ARE MISSING.  `modules/d01_crypt/resources` (116 rows) and
  its sibling level packs are the same story from the other side: 349,684-byte textures x22
  in one bundle, and every known texture of that size in the corpus is a `*_norm` map.  They
  are normal maps of bases we do not hold, and note the corpus spells the channel both ways
  (`blocks/d01_lava/lavastruct_lavaflow_normal` beside `..._norm`), so a channel sweep has to
  carry `normal`, `norm`, `diff`, `diffuse`, `spec`, `hm`, `m`, `illum`, `normal_aware`.

  68.5 WHY THE SUBSET SWEEPS ARE MOSTLY EMPTY - AND WHAT THAT MEANS.  A subset sweep only
  works if its flag vocabulary matches the chains.  Per technique it does not: `base` has 31
  flags, `character`/`mobile_filter`/`filter` 7 each, `billboard` 13 - so `base`'s own
  sorted-subset space at length 5 is only 169,911 candidates and is 0.  Yet the chains found
  here mix vocabularies (`character:double_sided:enemy:one_bit_alpha:skinned_3weights` is
  filter-flavoured flags under `character`; `base:instanced:diffuse_map` is instancing under
  `base`).  The follow-up therefore enumerates `<technique>:<sorted subset of the *global*
  152-flag vocabulary>` - `tools/chain_global_subsets.py`, chunked by technique and k because
  C(152,4) is 21 M and C(152,5) is 124 M.  Recorded so the next reader does not re-run the
  per-technique version and conclude the space is closed.

  State after this round: overlay 6,903 names, 906 -> 905 unique unresolved hashes.

==========================================================================
9H.7  MENU AND LEVEL-LOAD API - driving both from Lua
==========================================================================

None of this needed disassembly.  The game's own Lua does all of it, so the call
shapes below are copied from the shipped source rather than inferred, and the names
are confirmed against the captured runtime API (runtime_lua_api.tsv).  The reason to
have it written down: putting a level in front of the player without the campaign is
four calls, and every one of them is already in the game.

  RUNTIME WIDGET CREATION - lua/menu/screen_main_menu.lua:577-607
    The main menu builds its PC-only "change controller" button while it runs:
        local button_widget = GUI:load_proto(proto)
        self.widget:add_child(button_widget)
    proto = { id, selectable, style = "button_standard", type = "button", text,
              position = {"left + 180", "bottom - 40"} }.  For button_standard the
    `text` field alone renders the label - the larger menu buttons add a
    create_text_shadow_proto child for theirs, but that helper is a file-local of
    that file and is not reachable from a mod.  Note ScreenMainMenu.widget_clicked
    dispatches on widget.id and silently ignores ids it has no case for, so a custom
    id is inert until something handles it.

  LOADING A LEVEL - lua/states/menu_level_daemon.lua:126-157
    The menu's own background level, loaded on state_menu entry:
        proxy = WorldManager:new_world_proxy(WORLD_NAME, Application.DISABLE_PHYSICS,
                                             Application.DISABLE_APEX_CLOTH)
        proxy:set_use_engine_dt(true)
        viewport = proxy:create_viewport("menu_level_viewport_3d", "default")
        viewport:set_shading_environment(level_name)
        viewport:set_active(true)
        level = proxy:load_level(LEVEL_NAME, level_name, false)
        Level.trigger_level_loaded(level)
    This is the shortest Lua route to "the engine has loaded this level" - no lobby,
    no progression state, no level-select graph.  It loads into a world that already
    has a viewport and a camera, which is why it is the cheap way to see a level load.

  REACHING THOSE OBJECTS
    WorldManager.world_proxies is a plain table keyed by world name; while the menu
    is up, "menu_level_world" is already in it.  The MenuLevelDaemon instance is NOT a
    global (lua/boot/game.lua creates it as a local) but it is published at
    lua/boot/game.lua:420 as menu_manager.menu_level_daemon, and Game is a manager
    singleton, so Game.menu_manager.menu_level_daemon reaches it.

  STANDING THE DAEMON DOWN
    MenuLevelDaemon.update begins `if not self.level then return end`.  Clearing
    `level` is therefore the engine's own "nothing to do here" switch.  This has to
    happen before swapping the level underneath it: the daemon drives
    viewport.camera_unit every update, and a level swap may have freed that unit -
    a stale handle the engine would follow.

  STILL UNCONFIRMED
    Whether a level swapped in this way renders sensibly from the menu camera.  The
    menu camera is posed for the menu level's own camera_spline, and a gameplay room
    has no camera_spline, so the camera is wherever the menu put it.  Not yet
    established in-game: the mod written to try it (GauntletModLoader,
    mods/functions/CustomLevels) installs no hooks yet - see that repo's README for
    the exact state and the log evidence.

==========================================================================
9H.8  THE QUILT SYSTEM - and why 231 shipped pieces can never appear
==========================================================================

A crypt / caves / lava floor is not a level resource.  It is ASSEMBLED at load time
by the dungeon generator from a Lua layout table.  That is the whole explanation for
the odd result in 9H.7: no quilt piece is ever referenced by a .level - verified, 0
of 209 crypt quilt resources appear in any of the 255 extracted .level files.  The
layouts are the only place a piece is named, and they are Lua.

  lua/dungeon/layouts/<env>_floor_NN.lua    a plain Lua table:
                                              { path = "...",
                                                <group> = { <piece> = { supply = N } } }
  lua/dungeon/quilt_builder.lua:195         QuiltBuilder.parse_tiles reads it and
                                            matches each piece name against
      ^%w+_quilt_(%w+)__([^_]+)_([^_]+)_([^_]+)_([^_]+)__(.*)$
                   kind   up        right     down      left       variant
  lua/dungeon/dungeon_graph_builder.lua:416 QuiltBuilder(layout_node.quilt, seed)
  lua/dungeon/dungeon_generator.lua         walks the built clusters into the level

THE FOUR SIDE STRINGS ARE THE INTERFACE.  One character per tile, x = blocked,
e / o / i = opening, and the string length is that side's tile width.  So
xox_xi_xix_ix is a 3 / 2 / 3 / 2-tile room and xxoxx_xxxi_xxixx_ixxx is 5 / 4 / 5 / 4.
Two pieces with the same signature are interchangeable - which is what makes a piece
safe to add to a group, and what a layout's group names are describing.

  INVENTORY OF UNUSED CONTENT (whole-name match against every shipped layout)
    quilt pieces shipped            498
    named by some layout            267
    NEVER named by any layout       231     <- 46%

  by kind:  straight 81, turn 48, mediumroom 43, largeroom 16, smallroom 12,
            entrance 11, blackbox 11, encounter 3, exit 3, saferoom 3

  This is not filler.  It includes rooms built for named enemies that were never
  wired into any floor:
    caves_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xex_xix_xex__01_juggernauts
    caves_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xex_xix_xex__03_lobbers
    caves_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xex_xix_xex__03_spider_queen
    caves_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xex_xix_xex__03_spawner
    caves_quilt_largeroom__ooo_xox_iii_xix__01_keydoorseast_spider_queen
  plus three unused caves entrances (__01 / __02 / __03).

  DEAD BY CONSTRUCTION, not merely unused:
  caves_quilt_largeroom__xox_xex__xix_xex__02_spider has a doubled underscore
  (xex__xix) where the convention is single.  parse_tiles cannot match that name, so
  it could never be placed even if a layout listed it.  The other 230 were not
  checked for the same defect - worth doing before assuming they are all merely
  unwired.

  WHICH FLOORS ARE PROCEDURAL
    have a quilt table : crypt_floor_02, 05, 08 and every crypt_endless_* (the caves
                         and lava equivalents likewise)
    fixed_modules      : crypt_floor_01, 03, 04, 06, 07, 09, 10 and the six
                         colosseums - hand-built, no quilt table at all
  Overriding a fixed floor's layout with a quilt layout CONVERTS it into a generated
  floor, because floor_data then has root = "quilt_cluster" where it had
  fixed_modules.  That is a real change to those levels, not a swap of decoration.

  TESTING A PIECE IN-GAME
  Hook `require` and serve our own copy of the layout - see the loader repo,
  mods/functions/QuiltExtras, and 9H.7 for the call shapes.  A piece in its own group
  is placed as that group's role; a piece added to a group that already holds its
  signature needs nothing else.  Grouping by signature is the safe default: every
  list the generator draws from then holds interchangeable pieces, so it cannot pick
  one that does not fit its neighbours.

  PLACED AND CONFIRMED LOADING (crypt)
    crypt_quilt_encounter__xox_xi_xix_ix__01
    crypt_quilt_encounter__xxoxx_xxxi_xxixx_ixxx__01
    crypt_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xex_xix_xex__01
    crypt_quilt_smallroom__o_e_i_e__01_skeleton
    crypt_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xex_xix_xox__01_keyenemies  (+ _skeleton)
    crypt_quilt_straight__e_x_e_x__03_speartrapschain
    crypt_quilt_straight__e_xx_e_xx__02_spiketrapschain
  Full-environment sweeps - 38 crypt / 47 caves / 25 lava pieces, grouped by
  signature, with every campaign floor of each environment pointed at its
  environment's layout - produced NO CRASHES in game, but some pieces render broken.
  So a piece can be inert, render wrong, or crash, and only running it says which.

  CONFIRMED NEGATIVE
    crypt_quilt_encounter__xex_xex_xex_xex__01_bigcross crashes the floor generator
    when placed in the encounter set.  Its signature is four-way (every side xex),
    unlike every other encounter piece, and the export shows 2 helper entities
    skipped - and the helper units the exporter drops are the markers (spawn, nav)
    the generator may be relying on.  Removing it from the layout stops the crash.

  PROCESS NOTES THAT COST REAL TIME (worth not repeating)
    - A generated layout must be COMPILED before it is deployed.  A one-line lupa
      check catches it; two rounds were lost to malformed Lua reaching the game.
    - The deploy must be CONDITIONAL on that check.  Chaining `cp` after a Python
      step that printed a verdict instead of exiting non-zero deployed broken files
      twice.  Exit non-zero on failure and let `&&` do the gating.
    - Never splice text by offset.  Replacing one section invalidates the offsets of
      every section after it - that is what produced mismatched braces, twice.  Split
      at section headers, transform each section independently, rejoin.
    - Match piece names WHOLE (`^\s*<name> = `), never as substrings.  A substring
      test found four pieces "already used" because `..._xex__01` is inside
      `..._xex__01_fancy`, and wrongly skipped two the same way.
    - Groups can hold MULTI-LINE entries (`supply = math.huge`), so a line-wise
      rewrite orphans their inner lines.  Rebuild groups wholesale.

==========================================================================
9H.9  NAMES AND HASHES - what the quilt work needed, and what is still missing
==========================================================================

  WHAT THE LAYOUTS ASK FOR IS ALL NAMED
    Every quilt name any layout references resolves to a catalogue resource:
    266 asked for, 266 found.  Nothing in the quilt system is blocked by a missing
    name - the 231 unused pieces are pieces nobody names, not names nobody has.

  BUT 2,017 OF 20,004 RESOURCES HAVE NO NAME (by type)
    texture 672, animation 539, shader_library 311, material 187, particles 141,
    shader 64, unit 48, timpani_bank 37, level 12, flow 4, package 2
    The two that gate level work: 12 level resources and 48 units.

  THE ONE UNNAMED LEVEL WE COULD LOOK AT
    hash f6e003b8ed3e7eb7, the only one of the 12 that was ever extracted
    (unresolved/level/, 37,632 bytes): 363 entities, 24 distinct templates, and all
    24 templates resolve - crypt walls, borders, railings, a light, and a
    gameobjects/level_editor/unwalkable marker.
    WHAT IT IS IS NOT ESTABLISHED.  An earlier reading of "80% match with
    crypt_quilt_smallroom__o_e_i_e__01" was too strong: that percentage is over a
    five-template set, and a smallroom quilt has few enough pieces that any crypt
    room shares walls and borders with it.  Consistent with a small room or a quilt
    piece; proof of neither.

  CRACKING IT
    Resource names hash as MurmurHash64A(name, 0), lowercase, little-endian
    (hashcracker/README.md).  An implementation of that was self-checked against
    three catalogue name/hash pairs - all three matched - so from here any candidate
    list can be checked exactly and quickly in Python.
    A first sweep of 6,600 candidates - crypt_quilt_<kind>__o_e_i_e__<NN>[_variant]
    across five quilt directories, five kinds, twelve numbers and twenty variants -
    found nothing.  The name is outside that space.
    Better next step: the repo already has quilt-specific vocabulary builders
    (hashcracker/tools/make_quilt_vocab.py and make_quilt_codes.py), which means the
    real naming schema for this family was worked out before.  Feed that vocabulary
    in rather than hand-rolled combinations, and widen the space to include
    modules/d01_crypt/room_* and the other piece kinds.  A few million candidates is
    minutes in Python; hashcracker's GPU path is for billions.

  STILL OPEN, RECORDED SO THEY ARE NOT LOST
    - The malformed-name check is now done for every one of them.  Of the 231 unused
      pieces, 229 carry a signature that parses and would be usable if a layout named
      them; 2 do not:
        caves_quilt_largeroom__xox_xex__xix_xex__02_spider   (doubled underscore)
        caves_quilt_straight__xx_e_xx_e_06_civilization      (no __ before variant)
      All 231 are listed, split by the two cases, in Data/unused_quilt_pieces.txt.
    - Which pieces render BROKEN in game is not recorded.  The full-environment
      sweeps crashed on nothing, but some pieces render wrong, and only a run
      distinguishes inert / renders-wrong / crashes.  bigcross is the sole entry on
      the crashes side.
    - In crypt.lua the per-section piece counts sum higher than the 38 pieces the
      builder reported placing, which may mean some sections kept shipped pieces
      alongside ours.  Unreconciled.

9H.11 THE BUNDLE-LOAD HANG, MEASURED  *** SUPERSEDES 9H.10 ***
  The 9H.10 conclusion - "the loader registers bundle packages but never loads them, so a
  level-carrying bundle hangs the boot" - is WRONG.  It was inferred from log ordering.
  Instrumented, the truth is the opposite: the loader hands the package to the engine
  correctly and THE PACKAGE ITSELF NEVER FINISHES LOADING.

  HOW IT WAS MEASURED
    mods/functions/PkgTrace/script_main.lua hooks the game's own PackageManager (Lua only,
    no rebuild) and reports state only on change, capped at 12 lines - an earlier version
    logged on every frame and wrote 2487 lines, because PackageManager.update runs per
    frame.  Needle: "room_edit_test".

    The full journey of the RoomEditTest bundle's package, from one boot:

      [Bundles] Found bundle: RoomEditTest (pkg: resource_packages/custom/room_edit_test,
                hash: 75b743117d313683, resources: 9)
      [Bundles] Copied bundle -> contents/75b743117d313683
      [ResourceHooks] Bundle DB structure OK (version=2, entries=14564)
      [ResourceHooks] Patched bundle_database.data: 9 row(s) inserted for 1 bundle(s).

      [PkgTrace] load_package(resource_packages/custom/room_edit_test, flush=nil)
      [PkgTrace] state: has_loaded=false online=false still_loading=true
        ... 1237 more times, one per frame, 07:56:48 -> 07:57:08 (~62 fps)
      has_loaded=true   : 0 occurrences

    So, definitively:
      - the loader DOES register the bundle, copy it to contents/<hash>, and patch the DB;
      - Application.resource_package(<path>) DOES resolve it (hook logged "1 result");
      - the game's own PackageManager DOES ask for it: load_package(path, flush=nil);
      - the game loop is ALIVE at ~62 fps and calling update() the whole time;
      - ResourcePackage.has_loaded(package) NEVER returns true.
    The game waits for it forever: game_client.lua:398 and state_load_game.lua:131 call
    Game:are_all_packages_online(_G.COMMON_GAME_PACKAGES), which gates on that predicate.
    THAT is the hang.  It is not a loader timing problem, and no C++ change to the
    preflight would fix it - the package is already being asked for.

  WHY THE EARLIER LUA/TEXTURE BUNDLES "WORKED" AND A LEVEL BUNDLE HANGS
    Unresolved, but now bounded: a texture/lua bundle's package goes through this same
    queue, so either its contents load fast enough to satisfy has_loaded, or its resources
    are not streamed.  A level is the type that may carry streamed data - see below.

  CORRECTIONS TO EARLIER ASSUMPTIONS, ALL MEASURED
    - bundle_database.data is NOT a resource-name index.  All 14564 rows are
      { u32 flags | u32 len | "<16-hex bundle hash>" | u32 len | "<same>.stream" | 0x00 }
      i.e. a registry of content FILES (14564 rows over 89 shipped bundles ~= one row per
      resource, in TOC order).  No resource name appears in the DB at all - names resolve
      by hash through each bundle's TOC.  bundle_control.py's "rows per bundle" therefore
      counts file-rows, not name matches.
    - Duplicate (type, name) across bundles is NORMAL, not fatal.  Scanning shipped TOCs
      with a TOC-only scan (decompressing just the TOC region, not the payloads) shows the
      game ships the same level in up to three bundles at once:
        modules/d01_crypt/room_crypt_floor_01_adventure_template
            owners: 0164ad48615ba9ed
        modules/d01_crypt/prefabs/gate_exit/prefab_crypt_gate_exit_02
            owners: 0164ad48615ba9ed, 0f07a1cb5132485b, 3ee1f740a8e57e21
        modules/d01_crypt/prefabs/door_walls/prefab_crypt_door_wall_6c
            owners: 0164ad48615ba9ed, 0f07a1cb5132485b, 3ee1f740a8e57e21
      All 8 level resources in RoomEditTest are shipped names being overridden.  Precedence
      between bundles is therefore already a solved problem in this engine.
    - bundle_inspect.py CANNOT convict a bundle of being malformed.  It reports "TOC says
      X but the header says Y" for shipped bundles too - e.g. 11a95e9c862b533e (89
      resources) and 0bd097fa120382fe - so its payload-walk alignment model does not hold
      for all real bundles.  It parsed the 42-resource 2366c0121dc7d2de and the 1341-
      resource 3ee1f740a8e57e21 cleanly, but that is not a property we can rely on for a
      verdict.  Do not use its PROBLEM lines as evidence.
    - Shipped `level` resources inspected so far carry stream 0 (not streamed), yet their
      owning bundles DO ship .stream companions (0164ad48615ba9ed: 22.5 MB) - so streams
      exist for some resource types but not visibly for these levels.
    - The bundle is built with HasStream=false and a 0-byte .stream file.

  NEXT STEP (cheap, offline, one launch to answer)
    Fork it with one variable: build a minimal bundle carrying ONE level resource under a
    NEW name (a verbatim copy of a shipped level, no edit), so nothing is overridden.
      - if it comes ONLINE      -> the mechanism works; the defect is in overriding a
                                  shipped name or in the patched blob's size/records
      - if it also never loads  -> a level resource cannot be shipped in a mod bundle by
                                  this builder at all, and the container format needs work
    A second, independent probe: for the 8 names above, compare the owning shipped
    bundle's record dataSize against what RoomEditTest declares (manifest says 47,728 for
    room_crypt_floor_01_adventure and 396,608 for ..._template).  The DB carries no sizes,
    so this must come from the TOC/headers of the owning bundle - and the payload walk for
    that has to be solved properly first (see bundle_inspect above).

  TEST HYGIENE (unchanged, still holds)
    - steam -applaunch focuses an existing instance instead of starting a new one;
      kill with pkill -x gauntlet.exe first.
    - the loader rewrites modloader.log each run, so read it immediately after a run.
    - never log per frame from a hooked update(); cap and dedupe by state.


9H.12 THE CONTAINER LAYOUT WAS THE ROOT CAUSE  *** SOLVED, PROVEN IN GAME ***
  §9H.11 established that a `level` bundle's package never finishes loading.  This section
  is why, and the fix.  The bundle container the builder wrote was not in the engine's
  format at all: the engine could not read a single resource out of it.  The package's
  async load therefore never completed, ResourcePackage.has_loaded() stayed false forever,
  and the game hung on Game:are_all_packages_online(_G.COMMON_GAME_PACKAGES).

  THREE DEFECTS, ALL IN mod_bundle_builder.py's _build_bundle
    1. TOC had (N+1) entries - a repeated "sentinel" of entry[0].  The engine's table is
       exactly resource_count*16 and the payloads start immediately after it, so the extra
       16 bytes shifted every resource header.  The repair drops the sentinel.
    2. Resource 0 got a 20-byte header with NO type and name.  Every resource carries the
       same 36-byte header {u64 type, u64 name, u32 flags, u64 stream_size, u32 data_size,
       u32 unknown}, the first included.  With the 20-byte form the engine read resource 0's
       flags and sizes as its type and name and then walked off the end.
    3. Payloads were padded to a 4-byte boundary.  The engine advances to
       data_start + data_size with no alignment padding, so each pad byte shifted the next
       header.
  Each is silent - the sizes stay plausible and nothing reports an error.

  HOW IT WAS FOUND: THE REPO'S OWN DECODER, USED AS AN ORACLE
    analyze_package_dependencies.py already parses shipped packages correctly (13/13 on
    contents/b22560b172a4cff8), so it is ground truth that costs nothing to run:

      SHIPPED b22560b172a4cff8 : count=13 off=0x104 parsed=13 packages=13
      MINE (before)            : count=2  off=0x14  parsed=2  packages=0   <-- fell back
      MINE (after the fix)     : count=2  off=0x104 parsed=2  packages=1

    Before the fix its first candidate (0x104) yielded nothing and it fell back to a
    nonsense candidate, which is exactly what the engine was doing.  After, ours reads
    identically to a shipped bundle:
      [0] type 0xAD9C6D9ED1E5E77A name 0x169133D4E2CF14AD flags 1 main 25    (package)
      [1] type 0x2A690FD348FE9AC5 name 0xBD54B2FDE04C820A flags 1 main 1104  (level)
    Validate any future bundle change this way BEFORE launching the game.

  PROOF IN GAME (PkgTrace, mods/functions/PkgTrace/script_main.lua)
    LevelProbe, one 1,104-byte leaf level, NEW name, nothing overridden:
      [08:08:24] load_package(resource_packages/custom/level_probe, flush=nil)
      [08:08:24] state: has_loaded=false online=false still_loading=true
      [08:08:27] state: has_loaded=true  online=true  still_loading=false
    RoomEditTest, 9 resources, the Blender-edited room overriding shipped names:
      [08:09:33] load_package(resource_packages/custom/room_edit_test, flush=nil)
      [08:09:36] state: has_loaded=true  online=true  still_loading=false
    Both online.  The rebuilt RoomEditTest is 119,440 bytes against the old 119,456 - the
    difference is exactly the 16-byte sentinel that is no longer written.

  THE PACKAGE BLOB HAD A SECOND, INDEPENDENT DEFECT
    Package entries are {u64 type, u64 name, u32 dependencyCount, u32 deps[dependencyCount]}
    (analyze_package_dependencies.py:184, struct "<QQI" then dep_count*4).  The builder
    emitted a fixed "2, 0, name_hi32" triple - the same 28 bytes, so the sizes matched and
    nothing looked wrong - but the engine read it as dependencyCount=2 with dep[0] =
    0x00000000, a dependency on a resource hash that does not exist.  It now emits the real
    count.  Shipped entries all use 2, where dep[1] is the entry name's high 32 bits and
    dep[0] is a per-package constant (e.g. 0x82DE0B1B within contents/b22560b172a4cff8);
    custom bundles emit 0 dependencies for now, which the decoder accepts (status=ok).
    Whether the engine wants the two-dependency form for a resource with real dependencies
    is NOT settled - a unit referencing a material is the test that would settle it.

  THE TOOLING MODELS ARE WRONG AND MUST BE CORRECTED
    Both bundle_inspect.py and the loader's src/bundle_diagnostics.cpp assume
      - resource 0 has a 20-byte header with no type/name,
      - the TOC has (N+1) entries with a sentinel,
      - payloads are 4-byte aligned.
    All three are wrong, and that is why the loader reported our bundle as having "no
    structural problems" while the engine could not read it.  Until they are corrected they
    will keep clearing malformed bundles, so a bundle that passes them is not evidence.
    The correct model is in analyze_package_dependencies.py:parse_bundle.

  ALSO FIXED ALONG THE WAY
    bundle_control.py's strip filter was inverted (it kept the rows of the bundles being
    stripped and would have dropped the kept one's), so it printed "stripped: 0 row(s)"
    while leaving dangling rows pointing at content files it had just deleted.  It now
    strips by the hashes of the KNOWN bundles that are not wanted, and a --none/--only
    cycle moves the database cleanly (14564 -> 14553 -> ...).

  TOOLS THAT CAME OUT OF THIS
    - mods/functions/PkgTrace/script_main.lua - Lua-only trace of one package through the
      game's own PackageManager.  Reports state CHANGES only, capped, because
      PackageManager.update runs per frame; an uncapped version wrote 2487 lines.
    - scripts/level_probe_mod/mod.json - the minimal one-resource probe bundle.
    - scripts/bundle_control.py -now also the way to guarantee a single-variable test.

9H.13 THE MOD BUNDLE PATH WORKS END TO END  *** CONFIRMED IN GAME ***
    An edit to a shipped resource, shipped inside a mod bundle, IS what the engine uses.
    That was the last unknown: precedence over the games own copy of the same resource.

  THE PROOF
    A position patch to room_crypt_floor_01_adventure - every `gameobjects/gold/pile_small`
    entity raised 1.5 m - built into the RoomEditTest bundle and confirmed visible in the
    campaign's crypt floor 01.  The room ships inside contents/0164ad48615ba9ed, so this is
    an override winning, not a new resource appearing.

    The patch, for reference, since the axis is easy to get wrong: the .level file is Z-up
    and `up` is the THIRD float of the record's position (OFF_POSITION = 48, so +8).  The
    GLB exporter is what converts Z-up to Y-up; do not "raise in Y" in the level file.
    371 entities in that file, 152 matched, size unchanged, md5 1d34b622... -> dcda67a3...

  THE SECOND SILENT FAILURE: AN EDIT THAT IS NOT AN EDIT
    The first attempt changed nothing in game because the file listed as the edited level
    was BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pristine export - same md5, 0 bytes differing.  Everything
    downstream was happy: the container parsed, the package came online, the room rendered.
    It just rendered the original.

    So scripts/check_mod_sources.py now compares every resource a mod.json lists against
    the game's own copy of the same resource and reports which are unchanged:

        check_mod_sources.py room_edit_mod/mod.json

    Exit status 1 when the mod overrides shipped resources and none of them differ, i.e. it
    cannot change anything.  A mod of new names only is reported as nothing-to-compare.

  THE VERIFICATION CHAIN - run all three before any deploy
    1. check_mod_sources.py <mod.json>          -> at least one resource must differ
    2. analyze_package_dependencies.py <bundle> -> off must be 0x104, parse_status ok
    3. compare the md5 of the bundle's blob for the edited resource against the edited
       source file; it must match, and must differ from the pristine export
    Skipping step 1 cost a month.  Skipping step 3 is how a bundle ships the original.

  THE QUILTEXTRAS SCOPE BUG THIS SURFACED  (loader repo, mods/functions/QuiltExtras)
    QuiltExtras serves our own dungeon layout files in place of the game's, so shipped but
    unused quilt pieces can be exercised.  Its OVERRIDES table listed 63 layouts - and 39 of
    them are SCRIPTED floors, `fixed_modules` layouts whose whole purpose is to name the
    rooms they must contain.  Serving a quilt in their place DELETES those rooms:

      crypt_floor_01            room_crypt_floor_01_adventure   (the campaign's first room)
      crypt_floor_04 / 07       room_crypt_floor_02 / 05_adventure
      crypt_floor_10            room_crypt_floor_06_boss
      caves_floor_10            room_caves_floor_10_boss
      every colosseum arena     18 layouts (crypt, caves and lava x 6)

    Symptom: the campaign floor generated as a quilt with no adventure room, and no arena
    could appear, which also blocks the CustomColosseums mod - its arena layout is not the
    one that loads.  crypt_floor_01.lua is moreover the ONLY shipped layout that names
    room_crypt_floor_01_adventure, so overriding it removes the room from the whole game.

    Now restricted to the 15 endless layouts (crypt_endless_*, crypt_endless_start_*,
    caves_endless_*, lava_endless_*) - floors that exist to be procedurally generated, so a
    different quilt table changes their layout without removing anything the game needs.
    crypt_floor_02, 05 and 08 are quilt-built with no fixed_modules and are safe to add
    deliberately, but they are campaign floors and are out by default.

9H.14 CUSTOM GEOMETRY, AND THE PLAYER SPAWN  *** SPAWN MECHANISM SETTLED ***
    Two things came out of building a level of our own: how the engine places a player, and
    that loading a level into the live MENU world crashes this engine.

  THERE IS NO SPAWN-MARKER ENTITY
    A level cannot say where the player starts, because nothing reads such a marker.  The
    player is placed by a downward RAYCAST onto static geometry filtered "floor":

      lua/states/game_client.lua   local spawn_position = QueryManager:query_floor_enter_position(position)
                                   ... EntityAux.command_immediately(unit, "motion", "force_set_position", spawn_position)
                                       Unit.teleport_local_position(unit, 0, spawn_position)

      lua/managers/query_manager.lua:74
        QueryManager.query_floor_enter_position = function (self, position)
            local ray_position = position + Vector3.up() * 5
            local ray_direction = Vector3.down()
            local ray_length = 10
            local physics_world = self.world_proxy:get_physics_world()
            local any_hit, hit_position = PhysicsWorld.immediate_raycast(physics_world,
                ray_position, ray_direction, ray_length, "closest", "types", "statics",
                "collision_filter", "floor")
            return any_hit and hit_position or position
        end

    So the "spawn marker" is FLOOR-FILTERED COLLISION at the spot you want, and the only way
    to spawn at a chosen point is Lua (Unit.teleport_local_position - what the game itself
    uses).  A placed marker entity would spawn nothing.
    Practical consequence: a plane authored as floor collision IS a spawn surface.  The
    FlatPlaneTest plane (walked on, confirmed in game) is one, because the template's
    collision came along in the trailer.  Whether the raycast's "floor" filter accepts it
    is the one thing still untested, and it decides whether Lua is needed at all.
    The trailer carries `a_collision` but no filter NAME, so the filter is an enum and
    cannot be read offline.

  BUILDING OUR OWN LEVEL  (tools, all offline-verified)
    scripts/make_plane_glb.py        writes a flat quad as GLB in ENGINE space (XY plane,
                                     +Z normal) - glb_to_template_unit.py does no axis
                                     change, so a Blender export arrives standing on edge
    scripts/glb_to_template_unit.py  (existing) splices the mesh into a native unit and
                                     keeps that unit's TRAILER, which is what carries
                                     collision/physics (CUSTOM_ASSET_LOADING_FINDINGS 10.2).
                                     Our plane: 2568 B = 434 B mesh + 2134 B trailer.
    scripts/level_set_entities.py    replaces a level's entity table, keeping the header,
                                     config, lighting and every other section intact, and
                                     verifies that NOTHING else changed.  --replace-at N
                                     swaps one entity (keeps a room's props and its
                                     load-bearing encounter doors); --zero-sublevels drops
                                     the 56-byte sub-level placement records, which is how
                                     "no crypt shell" is built.
    mod_bundle_builder.py            now ships a prebuilt .unit AS-IS (rs["raw"] or a
                                     .unit source) - re-encoding dropped the trailer.

  PROVEN IN GAME (FlatPlaneTest, mods/bundles/FlatPlaneTest)
    A 40 m quad spliced into the ham template, replacing ONE entity in
    room_crypt_floor_01_adventure.  It loads, renders, and THE PLAYER CAN WALK ON IT - so
    the spliced unit's collision survives the whole path.  It renders with the template's
    own material (m_ham), which is expected: patch the unit footer hash to bind our own
    (10.3), no format work needed.

  LOADING A LEVEL INTO A LIVE WORLD CRASHES THE ENGINE - AND WHY THE BUTTON VANISHED
    The engine dies inside world:load_level.  Established by elimination, each a separate
    launch, all with step-by-step logging so the last log line names the call that did not
    return:
      - into the menu's own world (menu_level_world)          CRASH
      - into a brand-new world proxy of our own, the way the game's
        menu daemon creates one (new_world_proxy + create_viewport)   CRASH
      - our minimal one-entity level, and a pristine SHIPPED level    CRASH both
      - at frame 300 (menu long settled), not just during construction  CRASH
      - with viewport:set_shading_environment() added, as the daemon does  (untested)
    Four variables eliminated, so this is not the level, not the world, and not early
    timing.  A shipped, unmodified level dying there is the decisive one.

    THE MISSING BUTTON, separately: CustomLevels hooked ScreenMainMenu.rebuild_ui, but the
    menu had already built its UI by then, so add_button never ran - no error, no log, no
    button.  And the menu ACTIVATES that button by itself while booting (nothing clicks it),
    which fired the load and killed the boot.  Both are now handled: add_button also runs
    from a ScreenMainMenu.update hook (proven: "'Custom Levels' button added to the main
    menu"), and the first activation only ARMS - the load needs a second, real click.
    So the boot is stable with the mod deployed; what remains broken is the load itself.

    Note rebuild_ui calls GUI:destroy_widget(self.widget) and rebuilds the tree, so any
    child added to it is discarded on every rebuild - it has to be re-added, which is why
    the update hook matters and why "it appeared once and then vanished" is expected.

  NEVER CHANGE A LEVEL'S ENTITY COUNT
    Replacing a level's whole entity table crashes the level load when the CAMPAIGN loads
    it - a different crash from the load_level one below, and it cost one launch:

      room_crypt_floor_01_adventure            371 entities -> 371 (one replaced)   loads
      room_crypt_floor_01_adventure_template  4247 entities -> 1  (whole table)     CRASH

    The level's other structures still reference the original number of entities, so a
    table that no longer matches them is inconsistent and the load dies.  The safe form
    keeps the count EXACTLY: replace the entity you want with yours and move the rest out
    of the way instead of deleting them.  Specifically, for the shell:

      entity 0            -> our plane (position 57,52,10; scale 4 -> 160 m)
      entities 1..4246    -> position.z += 100000   (hidden, still present)
      count               4247 -> 4247, header[0] untouched

    Verified as: size unchanged, count field unchanged, 0 bytes differing outside the
    entity table - the same safety profile as the working room override.

  A CUSTOM UNIT'S PHYSICS COMES FROM ITS TEMPLATE'S TRAILER
    glb_to_template_unit.py keeps the template's trailing sections, and those carry the
    physics ACTOR.  Splice into a loose physics prop and your geometry becomes a dynamic
    body: the 400 m plane sliced into ham_plate fell and tumbled, which presented as "still
    far away" (it had fallen) and "rotated 30-45 degrees" (it had spun on the way down).
    Both symptoms, one cause, and nothing in the log said anything.

    So pick a template whose physics matches what the thing IS:
      a floor    -> gameobjects/procedural_floors/entrance_blocker  (trailer 2547 B)
      the ham_plate we started with is a dynamic prop                (trailer 2134 B)

    Trailer size is a decent tell that you got a different template; the actor type inside
    it is not readable offline, so pick by what the template IS in game.

    Related: this is the second symptom this session whose cause was a plausible-looking
    choice rather than a measured one (the first was centring the plane on the props' median
    instead of the room's origin).  The donor's own values are the ones to copy.

9H.15 WHERE STATIC COLLISION LIVES  *** OPEN - the map, and what is not known ***
    The question: a plane of our own that the player stands on and that does not move.

  SETTLED: UNITS DO NOT CARRY STATIC COLLISION
    Only 23 of the 582 extracted units contain an `a_collision` actor, and every one of them
    is a thing that MOVES: ham_plate, turkey_plate, carry/elemental_*, carry/carry_barrel_*,
    spawners/spawner_*.  A solid crypt WALL unit (props/d01_crypt/crypt_brick_02) has NO
    physics actor and no collision strings - only a shader name.  So walls are solid because
    the LEVEL says so, not because the unit does.  Hence a unit-based plane is inherently a
    simulated body, and there is no static template to borrow.

  THE HAM'S TRAILER EXPLAINS BOTH SYMPTOMS
      a_collision, angular_velocity, angular_velocity_random, apply_velocity,
      ham_plate, linear_velocity, linear_velocity_random
    A flow that applies RANDOM linear and angular velocity.  That is the tumbling, on top of
    the fall: not merely a dynamic body, one given a random shove and spin.

  AND WHY THE FLOOR UNIT WAS WORSE
    gameobjects/procedural_floors/entrance_blocker looked ideal and CRASHED the level load.
    All 23 collidable units carry flow data; that one's is procedural-floor logic and our unit
    inherited it.  Splicing into a collidable template imports its BEHAVIOUR, not just physics.

  THE SECTION MAP OF A .level  (16 offset/count pairs at the head)
    *** THE HEADER MODEL BELOW IS WRONG - see 9H.16: the 128 bytes are 32 uint32 fields,
        counts interleaved with offsets.  The section offsets in this map are still the
        real offsets; the (field_a, field_b) pairing is not. ***
    ROOM  room_crypt_floor_01_adventure (47,728 B, 371 entities) - DECORATION, no geometry:
      @0x80     entity table                371 x 72
      @0x7AD0   120 B   layer/shading config   'default', 'modules/d01_crypt/shading_environment'
      @0x7B48   8864 B  FlowCallbacks          'ability_name', 'encounter_node_disable'
      @0x9DE8   2292 B  flow-ish               'activate', a character path
      @0xAD00   392 B   the PLACEMENT table    7 x 56 - the records retargeted for our plane
      @0xAE88   2968 B  offset table           entries repeat a single offset
    SHELL  room_crypt_floor_01_adventure_template (396,608 B, 4247 entities) - SOLID:
      @0x80     entity table                4247 x 72 (~305,936 B)
      @0x4F8D0   120 B  layer/shading config   same shape as the room's
      @0x4F948  10944 B ?
      @0x52408    320 B all zeros
      @0x52548    528 B floats 1.0 / 0.0 - matrices?
      @0x52758    184 B zeros and small counts
      @0x52810    616 B 11 x 56-byte records, floats inside the room's own x range (-35..139)
      @0x52A78  23824 B NOT float data (read as float it spans 8e5)
      @0x58788      8 B two offsets
      @0x58790    112 B hashes, incl. 0x8bc6fb931657cc30 (gate_exit prefab)
      @0x58800  33976 B table of repeated self-referencing offsets
      @0x60CB8    136 B offsets
    The room being flows-and-placements only is consistent with it having no collision; the
    shell carries the bulk geometry, so the collision is one of its sections.

  WHAT IS NOT KNOWN
    WHICH section.  A float-span heuristic is not enough: several sections appear to span the
    room once read as floats, and 0x52A78's 8e5 span is garbage, i.e. not floats at all.  The
    next step is structural rather than statistical - find vertex/face structure (a vertex
    stride, a face count, a bbox per shape) and then TEST a candidate by locating this room's
    walls inside it, rather than by range alone.

  READY AND WAITING
    plane_mod/level_flat_plane.level - 1 entity, no sub-levels, donor header/config/lighting:
    a level containing nothing but our plane.  Its 3-resource package
    (resource_packages/custom/flat_plane_level) builds and validates offline (parse_status
    ok, 4911 B).  It needs a load route that does not crash, which currently means the
    campaign - i.e. overriding a shipped level - rather than a standalone world.

================================================================================
9H.16 THE LEVEL FILE, PROPERLY  *** Oct 2026 - the header model above was WRONG ***
================================================================================

9H.15 called the head "16 offset/count pairs".  It is not.  The 128 bytes are 32 uint32
fields: counts interleaved with offsets.  The old reading fitted the bytes and explained
nothing, which is why the section map could not be finished.

WHAT SETTLED IT
  reference/filediver/stingray/level/level.go  -  `RawLevel`, a sibling Stingray game's
  (Helldivers 2) level header: the same struct with 64 slots.  Its loader reads a count,
  SEEKS to an offset, and reads that many records; the file is a memory image with every
  pointer rebased to a file offset.  reference/filediver/patterns/level.hexpat is the same
  layout declaratively.  Gauntlet's build is older and has 32 slots, not 64.

  Everything below was then measured against three real levels:
    room_crypt_floor_01_adventure                47,728 B    371 units
    room_crypt_floor_01_adventure_template      396,608 B   4247 units
    level_24abcdf4b1fb4d4a (sample)               1,136 B      6 units

FIELD MAP  (verified fields only; the rest are unnamed offsets, not guesses)
  u32[0]  = 128        constant in the whole corpus - the unit table starts here
  u32[1]  = unit count  371 / 4247 / 6
  u32[2]  = data count  always 1
  u32[3]  = offset      24 bytes past the unit table
  u32[10] = offset      a 12-byte-record array
  u32[11] = count       its count (42 in the room, 14 in the shell)
  u32[14] = offset      the METADATA list
  u32[15] = count       a 56-byte-record array's count (11 in the shell)
  u32[16] = offset      that array
  u32[24] = offset      a PER-UNIT array: exactly 8 bytes per unit (371*8, 4247*8)
  u32[28] = file size - 8
  u32[29] = count       the PLACEMENT table's count (7 in the room, 2 in the shell)
  u32[30] = offset      that table, 56-byte records
  The three invariants that must hold - unit table at 128, it fits, data count is 1 - pass
  on all 11 .level files in this repo.

METADATA, FULLY DECODED  (at u32[14]; this was 9H.3's "resource config section")
  u32 count, then per entry:  u32 hashCount, u32 thinHash[hashCount], u32 type, value
  type 1 = uint32, 2 = float32, 3 = string (u32 length INCLUDING the null, then the bytes,
  padded to a 4-byte boundary).  It is `parseMetadataEntry` from filediver, exactly.
  Every level here carries 4 entries; the room's are
      0xf01676fc float32 1.0
      0xfe73c7dc string  'modules/d01_crypt/shading_environment'
      0xd8b27864 string  ''
      0xfe379225 string  'default'
  So 9H.3's "resource path / layer name config section" was the metadata list all along, and
  the padding rule it noted (strings padded to 4) is what a naive parse desyncs on: the
  empty string is 1 byte of payload padded to 4, and without the pad the next entry is read
  from the wrong place.

A UNIT RECORD, 72 bytes at u32[0], u32[1] of them - unchanged from 9H.2, re-verified
  +0 float flag, +4 scale xyz, +16 unused, +24 entity id, +32 template hash,
  +40 material override, +48 position xyz, +60 rotation xyz.

THE TOOL
  scripts/level_decode.py  - reads only; prints the header, the units, the metadata, the
  placements and every section with its size and content profile.  It asserts the three
  invariants, so a file that stops fitting the model says so instead of printing a
  confident wrong answer.  resource_tools/level_tools.py keeps encode/decode for editing.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9H.16.1 THE UNIT TRAILER, DECODED - and what it does NOT contain
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  A unit is [header][mesh][materials][TRAILER][footer].  The trailer is a byte-packed data
  table, and its records are the same shape as the metadata's:
      { u32 key_thin_hash, u32 offset, u32 type, u32 size, value }
  Read off two real units (extracted from bundle 0164ad48615ba9ed):

    blocks/common/invisible_wall_2c   (the collision wall)
      nav_grid_type_auto     = 'blocker'        (8-byte string)
      nav_grid_type_override = 'blocker'
      walkable / unwalkable  = arrays (type -1)
      material               = 'm_invisible_wall'
      footer                 = its own path hash
    blocks/d01_crypt/floors/cryptsmooth_floor_4c_4c   (a floor block)
      nav_grid_type_auto     = 'floor'
      nav_grid_type_override = 'floor'
      material               = 'm_cryptsmooth_floor_01'
      footer                 = blocks/d01_crypt/d01_crypt_swatch

  `nav_grid_type_auto` is the key lua/dungeon/nav_grid.lua reads -
  `Unit.get_data(unit, "nav_grid_type_auto") or "ignore"` - and nav_grid returns 'blocker'
  and 'ignore' as its own type names, so the decode is confirmed against shipped Lua, not
  merely self-consistent.  These are AI NAVIGATION values: "floor" here means "AI may walk
  on it", not "the player's floor raycast hits it".

  NEITHER UNIT HAS A PHYSICS ACTOR.  Neither does any structural block.  Of the 691
  extracted .unit files exactly 24 contain `a_collision`, and every one is a gameobject that
  MOVES: spawners/spawner_*, carry/carry_barrel_*, carry/elemental_*, food/ham_plate,
  food/turkey_plate.  A crypt wall, floor, border, pillar, stair, railing or rock has none.

      *** THIS PARAGRAPH IS WRONG - CORRECTED IN 9H.19.  A crypt wall, floor and pillar DO
      have physics actors, and 9H.19 proves it from the game's own build script.  What the
      search above actually found is a FLOW parameter: `a_collision` is a string inside the
      unit's flow data (it names an actor for FlowCallbacks.create_actor), which is why only
      the gameobjects that MOVE carry it.  The physics actors store no name strings, so no
      string search could ever find them.  Read 9H.19 before using anything in this
      subsection. ***

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9H.16.2 WHERE THAT LEAVES THE COLLISION QUESTION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  `blocks/common/invisible_wall_2c` is a COLLISION unit by name and by purpose - the shell
  places 93 of them - and its unit carries no actor at all.  So an invisible collision wall
  exists in a level without any unit-side physics definition, which means the engine must be
  building it.  Two engine strings agree:

    "Dynamic physics enabled for an object that is linked in the scene graph. This does not
     make sense. Unit: %s, Actor: %s"
    "Invalid actor \"%s\" in %s: Collision is not supported for dynamic triangle meshes,
     height fields or planes. Make the shape static or disable collision for it."

  i.e. level-linked units are expected to carry STATIC physics, and triangle meshes and
  height fields are named as real shapes.  The working hypothesis is therefore:

    a level-placed unit gets a STATIC collider from its MESH, and a unit that ALSO defines
    an actor (ham_plate and friends) gets that actor too - which is why the ham-templated
    plane tumbles while a wall stands still.

  That is testable, and it is the cheap test: splice the plane into a BLOCK unit - one with
  no actor definitions, e.g. blocks/d01_crypt/floors/cryptsmooth_floor_4c_4c - instead of
  ham_plate.  If the plane comes out static and walkable, 9H.15's "there is no static
  template to borrow" is answered by its inverse: the static templates are the ones with no
  actors.

  NOT established: that EVERY level unit is solid.  props/d01_common/fog_volume and
  gameobjects/level_editor/unwalkable are placed units that should not be, so a per-unit or
  per-mesh marker probably exists as well.  The block-versus-ham test settles the mechanism
  either way; the marker question is what to look at if the plane is solid but something
  else is unexpectedly solid too.

  RULED OUT, each with evidence:
    * actors in unit trailers      - the whole block family has none (9H.16.1)
    * flow in the level            - neither level contains 'create_actor', 'a_collision'
                                     or 'physics'; only the room has flow node names
    * a level 'physics' resource   - the type exists, but no name in the 147,168-entry name
                                     database is a physics resource belonging to a level
    * a baked collision mesh       - no section of either level holds a long run of
                                     coordinate-like floats (the longest run that is not
                                     zeros is 49 floats, in a 44%-zero section)

================================================================================
9H.17 THE BINARY SIDE  *** Oct 2026 - gauntlet.exe, via Ghidra ***
================================================================================

  Read with Ghidra 12.1.4 (tools/ghidra_12.1.4_PUBLIC + tools/jdk) against the existing
  ~/tools/ghproj/Gauntlet.gpr project.  Scripts live in scripts/ghidra/ and are run
  headless; see that directory's README-style headers for the exact command line.  Note
  that Ghidra 12 runs .py through PyGhidra, so either install the Jython extension (it
  ships as an uninstalled zip under Extensions/Ghidra/) or launch through
  Ghidra/Features/PyGhidra/support/pyghidra_launcher.py -H.

THE LEVEL PARSER, LOCATED
    World.load_level binding       FUN_004f2520   thin: builds a pose, calls ->
    FUN_00428a50                   allocates 0x208 (520 B) and calls ->
    FUN_0042b6b0                   *** the level loader ***
  FUN_0042b6b0 builds the Level object: it reads the resource, sizes arrays from the
  counts, copies the unit table, then walks the placement table.  It is also its own
  caller - a placed sub-level is loaded by recursing into it.

  This is where the header model stops being an inference.  The loader reads:
      +4        the unit count, used to size an array
      +0x1c     u32[7]  - a pointer to a structure
      +0x28     u32[10] - a pointer to a structure
      +0x34     u32[13] - a pointer/offset
      +0x74     u32[29] - THE PLACEMENT COUNT
      +0x78     u32[30] - THE PLACEMENT TABLE OFFSET
  and the sub-level loop advances by 0x38 = 56 bytes, reads the record's own resource id
  from +8, and builds a matrix from +16, +28 and +44.

9H.17.1 THE PLACEMENT RECORD, NOW FULLY DECODED (56 bytes)

    +0    8   instance id
    +8    8   resource id  = { MurmurHash64A of the name, type hash }
    +16  12   position     (3 floats)
    +28  16   rotation     (4 floats - a real quaternion, w included)
    +44  12   scale        (3 floats)

  The loader XORs +0 with the level's own id (two u32s) to make a unique instance id, so
  the raw value in the file is not the runtime one.

  VERIFIED on the room's own 7 records - all 7 resolve to real names, and the transforms
  are clean:
    [0..5]  modules/d01_crypt/prefabs/door_walls/prefab_crypt_door_wall_{10c,6c,10c,16c,6c,8c}
            at (56,-6,10) (48,6,10) (40,34,10) (128,94,4) (72,-6,10) (84,60,7)
            rotation (0,0,0.707107,-0.707107) = 90 deg, or (0,0,0,1) = identity
    [6]     modules/d01_crypt/room_crypt_floor_01_adventure_template   <- THE SHELL
            at the origin, identity, scale 1 - exactly what 9H.5 said the room places.
  So the room-to-shell link is now readable AND writable by name.  level_decode.py prints
  it in this shape; its earlier "u64 hash + 6 floats" reading was wrong (the floats it
  showed, e.g. -8.47e35, were the resource id's type hash being read as a float).

9H.17.2 THE LOADER CREATES NO PHYSICS  (this is a real negative)

  FUN_0042b6b0's callees are FUN_00402e10, FUN_0040a350, FUN_00453840, FUN_00453a20,
  FUN_00457bd0, FUN_005fea30, FUN_005ff140, FUN_0062a040, FUN_00636330, FUN_00642b00,
  FUN_006880e0 and itself.  No physics factory, no actor creation, no collision setup.
  Consistent with 9H.16.2: the file has no collision to load.

9H.17.3 HOW AN ACTOR IS INSTANTIATED - and why blocks never get one

    Unit.create_actor (Lua)     FUN_004e7ed0   thin: hashes the name, calls ->
    FUN_0045a890                looks up an actor by index in the unit's own array
        actor array   at [unit_resource + 0xc4]
        actor count   at [unit_resource + 0xbc]
    if the slot is empty it instantiates either from the resource's actor table
